#2131. Michael Olise Gives Chelsea A Hard Xabi Alonso Wide-Player Benchmark

Michael Olise has moved from former Chelsea academy footnote to genuine Ballon d’Or conversation piece.

That should land at Cobham as more than a wistful what-if.

Bavarian Football Works relayed comments from former Chelsea and France winger Florent Malouda, who described the Bayern Munich forward as a live Ballon d’Or contender if France turn their World Cup run into silverware.

The point is not simply that Chelsea once had Olise in the building. It is that his rise provides a hard benchmark for the type of final-third specialist Xabi Alonso must either develop or buy.

Chelsea have spent heavily on attacking upside. Olise’s rise is a reminder that the real separator is not potential, but repeatable elite production.

Bayern’s own season review underlines why the conversation has accelerated.

The German champions confirmed Olise finished 2025/26 with 25 goals and 28 assists from 57 competitive appearances. He was also named Bayern’s Player of the Season for a second straight year, finishing ahead of Harry Kane and Luis Diaz in the fan vote.

That is elite production from a wide creator who does not play like a chalk-on-boots winger.

Olise can receive wide, drift inside, manipulate the half-space and still deliver the last pass at match-winning speed. Chelsea have bought attackers with pieces of that profile, but few have joined the dots with that level of consistency.

There is an obvious recruitment lesson here.

The market will now price that blend of creativity, ball security and goal threat close to superstar territory. If Chelsea want an Alonso attack that controls games rather than simply runs through them, the next winger decision cannot be judged on pace or upside alone.

It has to be judged on game control, final-third calm and repeat output.

The World Cup has sharpened the comparison.

Chelsea’s official France preview noted that Malo Gusto was in the same squad as N’Golo Kante and former Blues academy player Olise. It was a neat reminder of how many Chelsea strands sit inside Didier Deschamps’ group.

Bundesliga’s tournament analysis was even more revealing. It noted that Olise had reached 10 France goal involvements in 18 appearances and was named Player of the Match on his World Cup debut against Senegal.

That same analysis highlighted the tactical adjustment that moved Olise closer to Kylian Mbappe after half-time. His passing range then became the lever that changed the game.

That matters for Chelsea because Alonso’s best Bayer Leverkusen sides were built around players who could receive between lines without killing the tempo. Cole Palmer already gives Chelsea one version of that.

The wider question is whether the squad has another attacker who can bend matches from the opposite side without turning every possession into a one-v-one.

Olise’s Chelsea connection can easily become sentimental. The sharper editorial point is structural.

This is the calibre of player who once passed through the academy pathway and later became a global-level attacker elsewhere. Chelsea cannot change that history, but they can use it to stress-test the next wave of decisions.

That matters for Tyrique George, Kendry Paez, Estevao and any new wide signing.

ReadChelsea has already looked at how Tyrique George’s likely Everton return gives Alonso a fresh winger audit. Olise’s current standing raises the standard of that discussion.

He is no longer just a former academy name or a missed Premier League target. He is evidence of what happens when technical security, role clarity and patience meet elite coaching.

For Alonso, that is the real value of the Olise noise.

Chelsea do not need to turn every academy exit into regret. They do need to recognise the standard now being set by one of Cobham’s former pupils.

The next wide forward who gets serious minutes at Stamford Bridge has to be assessed against that ceiling, not against the safer language of potential.

#2129. Chelsea Could Claim Share of £164m Compensation

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Chelsea fans have followed Manchester City’s financial rules case for years. Everyone at Stamford Bridge knows a guilty verdict could cost City serious money, and much of it could end up with rivals lower down the table.

Reports now suggest an independent panel has reached a decision. If that holds up, the scale of any Chelsea Manchester City compensation payout could run into tens of millions of pounds for the Blues alone.

The Premier League charged Manchester City with 115 alleged breaches of financial regulations in February 2023. That followed a four-year investigation into the club’s accounts. The case has hung over the competition ever since. Every top-flight club knows the outcome could change how recent Premier League seasons are remembered.

This matters directly to Chelsea. If City are found to have broken spending and disclosure rules, clubs who finished below them could argue they lost out on prize money and European qualification. Chelsea have competed directly with City for top four places and cup competitions throughout that period.

According to The Athletic, Manchester City were found guilty on all but one of the 115 charges by the independent panel. The Premier League has not confirmed the verdict or issued a statement.

Manchester City have not accepted any suggestion of guilt. The club released a statement addressing the ongoing process directly.

“The Premier League process remains ongoing, with significant elements to be completed, and subject to strict confidentiality. As such, Manchester City FC’s position remains consistent with the Club’s statement of February 2023. ‘The Club has diligently respected due process for eight years on the basis that the Premier League Board and Executive would behave as an independent, impartial and fair-minded regulator, free from partisan influence.'”

That statement makes clear the club does not see the case as settled. Until the Premier League confirms a result, the reported verdict will linger on for official sentencing.

Data specialists at OLBG have estimated the potential Chelsea Manchester City compensation windfall if City are found guilty. They believe City could owe domestic rivals well over £500m in lost Premier League prize money if the charges are proven.

Within that total, London clubs are said to be due around £164m between them. Chelsea, Arsenal and Tottenham Hotspur are among the clubs who could be entitled to a share. Other Premier League sides could benefit too, depending on where they finished compared with City in the seasons in question.

Prize money in the Premier League is tied closely to league position. If City gained points unfairly, that would have pushed a rival down the table and cost them money too. That is the basis for the OLBG figures, and it explains why the sums involved are so large.

For Chelsea, even a share of that £164m pot would be a big boost. It could help fund new signings, or simply ease the pressure around the kind of transfer valuations at Stamford Bridge the club faces every window.

City chairman Khaldoon Al Mubarak has already made clear the case is far from finished. He described the process as having “a long way to run.” That points to appeals and further legal steps, whatever the panel has decided.

Chelsea, for their part, carry on with life under Xabi Alonso regardless of how the case unfolds. Attention stays on squad rotation and Xabi Alonso’s squad management strategy, not on any potential windfall. The financial case will not change anything on the pitch in the short term.

The Premier League has stayed silent on the reported verdict, and confidentiality remains strict around the process, according to reports from Football.London. Chelsea fans should expect a long wait before any compensation, if it comes at all, reaches the club’s accounts.

#2126. Jamie Gittens suffers Chelsea injury blow with England U21s

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Jamie Gittens has withdrawn from the England Under-21 squad. He suffered an ankle injury during Tuesday’s qualifier against Kazakhstan at Molineux. The Chelsea winger left the ground on crutches. He has returned to Stamford Bridge, where the Jamie Gittens injury will now be assessed by the club’s medical staff.

Gittens picked up the knock after a challenge during the second half of England’s win over Kazakhstan. He was withdrawn as a precaution. He did not take any further part in the match. England have been careful not to overstate the issue and no further reports have come out further.

The 21-year-old struggled to put weight on the affected ankle and was seen limping around Molineux after full time. That prompted an immediate release back to his club, rather than any attempt to assess him further in camp.

England Under-21 boss Lee Carsley addressed the injury directly after the game. He confirmed the nature of the knock and the decision to send Gittens home.

“Jamie got a kick on his ankle,” Carsley said after the match. “We never take any risks. With the age of these players, we don’t ever keep them in camp to see how they are. He will go back.”

England’s statement on the squad changes, detailed in a report from Football.London, confirmed the wider fallout from the fixture.

“Mukasa’s call-up follows the withdrawal of Chelsea’s Jamie Gittens and Norwich City pair Kellen Fisher and Ben Chrisene through injury,” the statement read. West Ham midfielder Divine Mukasa has been called up as cover for all three.

Gittens has been an unused substitute in every Premier League fixture Chelsea have played this season, with his only two appearances under Xabi Alonso coming in the Carabao Cup. Those minutes have offered him little platform after his big summer move.

Alonso’s squad selection has been a talking point since he took charge. Our earlier look at Xabi Alonso’s squad choices at Stamford Bridge covered how competitive things have become. An ankle injury will not change his pecking order in the short term. It does, though, remove any chance of him pushing his case this week.

Chelsea’s break has brought other headlines too. Cole Palmer also withdrew, for entirely different reasons, after his recall by senior boss Thomas Tuchel to the full England squad. Chelsea will be watching both returns closely before domestic football resumes.

Chelsea have not yet issued a formal update on the severity of the injury. Any recovery timeline will depend on an assessment by the club’s medical team once Gittens is back at Cobham. Nothing beyond the crutches seen at Molineux has been confirmed.

Given his limited game time this season, the immediate concern is less about missed fixtures than a setback to his integration under Alonso. Fans can follow how the squad shapes up before the next fixtures through our recent line-up updates. Further news on Gittens is expected once Chelsea’s medical staff finish their evaluation.

#2125. Manu Koné: Roma slap £86m price tag on Chelsea target

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Roma have put an £86m (€100m) price tag on Manu Koné, according to Gazzetta dello Sport, reviving the Manu Koné Chelsea transfer link that first emerged over the summer. Roma want any interested club, including Chelsea, to know the price before serious talks can even begin.

Koné remains under contract at the Stadio Olimpico, and he has become a key part of Roma’s midfield since his move from Borussia Mönchengladbach. The Gazzetta report reads like a warning to potential buyers rather than proof of any talks between the two clubs.

Roma sporting director Tony D’Amico has confirmed Chelsea made contact late in the summer window, though he was quick to draw a clear line under how far things went.

“There was an inquiry and some preliminary contact with Chelsea on the final day of the transfer window. No official offers arrived that would allow us to approach the player. Something was imagined that simply didn’t happen. There was no official offer; the final day of the transfer window got everyone a little worked up.”

D’Amico’s comments confirm Chelsea looked at signing Koné before the deadline, but they stop well short of showing an official bid was ever made. He also explained why Roma had no wish to sell a key player so close to the window closing.

“Before the match against Fiorentina (on 24 August), I said it would be difficult for us to deprive ourselves of important players so near to the end of the transfer session, and so it proved. Kone is an important player, we’re happy he stayed with us, and we’re confident he’ll give his contribution during the season.”

Chelsea’s midfield has changed shape under Xabi Alonso since the summer, with Morgan Rogers arriving to add depth and competition for places. That signing came during a window that also saw exits acceleration under Xabi Alonso, as the head coach reshaped his squad around his own style.

Koné’s ball winning and his range of progressive passing would suit Alonso well, should Chelsea ever revisit their interest. Supporters have already seen how Alonso rotates his midfield for the biggest games, including the recent derby line-up selections against Brentford.

For now, Rogers and Chelsea’s existing central options give Alonso enough cover without needing an immediate signing. Roma’s reluctance to sell also means any deal would need patience rather than a quick resolution.

Nothing in D’Amico’s comments suggests Roma are open to a sale in the near term, and Koné has given no sign that he wants to leave. The £86m valuation, reported by Football London, stands as Roma’s marker rather than an asking price involved with Chelsea.

Will Chelsea return with a more formal approach in a future window? D’Amico’s comments give no hint either way. The Manu Koné Chelsea transfer link is unlikely to fade given Chelsea’s history of scouting France’s leading midfielders, but any fresh move will have to wait for Roma’s stance to soften.

#2124. “How are you feeling?” Long-suffering fans offered chance to make voices heard | West Ham News

About this time of the season West Ham fans will be realising – if they hadn’t done so before – that the Championship is a very different beast to the Premier League and the 46-game calendar is a marathon for each and every participant, each club on their day capable of beating the others. Predictable it is not.

West Ham sit currently outside the promotion places, one manager down and with new appointee Scott Parker desperately hoping to steady the ship during the international break. It’s been something of a rollercoaster for the 50,000 supporters who signed up for season tickets in the summer – many, anecdotally, on the back of a wave of optimism generated by David Sullivan’s resignation.

#2123. Joao Pedro Injury: Chelsea Forward Misses Brentford Defeat

Graeme Hanna is the site lead of Read Rangers and also writes about West Ham for Read West Ham. A Rangers season-ticket holder and freelance writer, he has previously written for Vital Rangers, Rangers Review, Football Ground Guide and Give Me Sport, and has a long association with the Follow Follow fanzine.

Joao Pedro missed Chelsea’s 3-0 Premier League defeat to Brentford with a knee problem, and the injury has since ruled him out of Brazil’s squad for the friendlies against Australia and India.

He was absent from Xabi Alonso’s confirmed lineups against Brentford, with Chelsea turning to other forward options instead.

Brazil’s coaching staff confirmed the withdrawal, pointing to the same knee issue that kept him out of the derby. Chelsea have not set a firm return date for Joao Pedro, leaving supporters to wait for further updates during the international break.

The Joao Pedro injury situation took a new turn when the Standard reported that he is seeing a specialist during the international break to speed up his recovery.

According to the Standard, the forward is using this pause in the calendar to get ahead of the problem rather than wait for Chelsea to resume domestic action.

Chelsea have not issued their own statement on the specialist visit, and the club’s official line on Joao Pedro’s fitness remains limited to his withdrawal from matchday duty.

Full detail on the reported treatment can be found in football.london’s reported injury update, which first carried the specialist claim alongside Xabi Alonso’s comments on the striker’s season.

Until Chelsea confirm otherwise, the Standard’s report stands as the clearest picture of how the club are handling the knee concern.

It suggests a measured approach, with recovery prioritised over rushing Joao Pedro back into contention for the next fixture.

Before the injury forced him out, Xabi Alonso had made his admiration for Joao Pedro clear. The Chelsea manager ranked the forward among the Premier League’s elite strikers, alongside Manchester City’s Erling Haaland.

“If you count how many top strikers we have in the Premier League, I’m sure that João is there, Haaland for sure is there.

“So I would rate him that way. He has a very strong goal this year: to have a great season, to score many goals, and to be a nominee for not just this month but more months. I am not surprised, but really happy with the kind of player that we have here with João.”

With Joao Pedro sidelined, Alonso must lean again on the depth built into his squad.

His approach to rotation, part of the squad management this season, faces another test while his in-form forward is unavailable.

#2122. Pep Guardiola Speaks Out as Man City Found Guilty of 114 Charges

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester City have been found guilty of 114 financial charges, the Premier League has confirmed. The verdict follows an eight-week hearing that opened almost two years ago. It closes one of the most significant disciplinary cases in the competition’s history.

Former head coach Pep Guardiola has already broken his silence. Reaction to the Pep Guardiola Man City charges verdict is spreading fast across the division, with Chelsea among the clubs watching closely.

Guardiola joined Manchester City in 2016 and turned them into the dominant force in English football. He left his post earlier this summer, with the club having already worked through plans to replace Pep Guardiola at City.

His exit means he holds no formal role at the Etihad. Yet he moved quickly to make his loyalty clear once the ruling landed. According to the football.london report that first carried his statement, Guardiola posted directly to his supporters.

“I want every single @ManCity fan to know I am here; more than ever. I am, always have been, always will, be behind my club, my owner, my chairman [Khaldoon Al Mubarak], Ferran [Soriano], the players, staff, Enzo [Maresca] and you, our unique supporters. Let me be clear: we will get through this together. I love you all.”

Manchester City chief executive Ferran Soriano has confirmed that the club intends to appeal the verdict. The statement from Guardiola comes to City’s defence despite him leaving the role this summer. He is still viewed as the man who built this era of success, even though he no longer sits in the dugout at the Etihad.

The Pep Guardiola Man City charges case matters just as much to Chelsea as it does to Manchester City themselves. Chelsea, along with Arsenal and Tottenham, are among the Premier League clubs directly affected by the outcome. Every point City may have gained through breaches has a knock on effect further down the table.

Most top flight clubs including Chelsea are now pushing for City to face relegation as a consequence of the verdict. Some are reported to be weighing up legal action of their own. Guardiola has spoken about Chelsea before, including Guardiola’s previous comments on Chelsea. This latest statement fits a pattern of public backing for Manchester City, despite the criticism now directed at the club.

For Chelsea supporters, the case is not just about points on a table. It touches on the integrity of seasons they competed in directly. That is why so many rivals are refusing to let the matter rest with a guilty verdict alone.

Manchester City’s appeal process is now the next stage in a case that has already run for nearly two years. Ferran Soriano has made clear the club will contest the findings. No date has been confirmed for when that appeal will be heard.

Until then, no points deductions, penalties or changes to past titles have been confirmed by the Premier League. Chelsea and their rivals will be watching the appeal timeline just as closely as they watched the original hearing. The final outcome could still be a long way off.

#2120. Alex Scott Chelsea Transfer Blocked as Bournemouth Eye January Stay

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Bournemouth will block any Alex Scott Chelsea transfer in January. That is according to reports from the Daily Mail and BBC Sport. Both outlets say the Cherries want to keep their young midfielder as they fight to protect their Premier League status this season.

Chelsea have been linked with serious interest in the 22-year-old. Reports suggest the Blues have already tested Bournemouth’s resolve with a bid worth close to £64 million. Bournemouth have given no indication they are willing to negotiate. Scott, the reports say, simply is not for sale this winter.

Reports in the Daily Mail and on BBC Sport state that Bournemouth have no intention of sanctioning a sale this winter. The club’s hierarchy reportedly view Scott as central to their survival plan, wary of leaning on new signings mid-season rather than trusting the squad they already have.

A fee of around £64 million, first reported by Football London, is said to have been offered by Chelsea during recent discussions.

Scott joined Bournemouth from Bristol City in 2023. He has quickly become one of the most sought-after young midfielders in English football. Interest from a club the size of Chelsea shows how far his reputation has grown, though Bournemouth’s stance makes a January move increasingly unlikely.

Scott’s rise has been underlined by his first appearance for England. He came off the bench for a 20-minute cameo as Thomas Tuchel’s side beat Czechia 2-0 in the Nations League. The manager praised him afterwards.

“Alex Scott has maybe one of the best performances in midfield in the Premier League since the start with Bournemouth.”

“Individually he’s one of the outstanding midfielders so he clearly deserves to be with us.”

“He took another step into the right direction. Very impressive and happy that he is on that route. Now he plays in Europe for Bournemouth and deserves to be with us.”

That praise from their former manager will only increase Chelsea’s interest. Scott’s energy and composure suit the identity Xabi Alonso wants to build at Stamford Bridge. The Spaniard has already shown he is willing to rotate his squad, as seen in Xabi Alonso’s squad management decisions this season.

Chelsea travel to face Bournemouth in the Premier League soon. It is a fixture that will give Alonso a close look at a player repeatedly linked with his club. Build-up to that game is already underway, with team news arriving ahead of the Bournemouth clash.

The prospects of an Alex Scott Chelsea transfer happening in January now look slim. Supporters hoping for fresh competition in central midfield may need patience, with Chelsea more likely to revisit their interest when the summer window opens.

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Noni Madueke has missed another England opportunity, despite making an encouraging impression during his recent run in the Arsenal starting line-up.

Thomas Tuchel has named his 25-man squad for the Nations League fixtures against Croatia and Spain in November, but Arsenal winger Madueke misses out again after failing to make the cut following a missed international camp.

He has been left out despite making the most of Bukayo Saka’s absence to increase his involvement under Mikel Arteta.

Madueke scored in the recent Premier League win against Nottingham Forest and then again in Wednesday’s Champions League win against Slavia Prague.

He had a legitimate claim to be named in the squad, and Tuchel admitted that Madueke was very close to being included.

The England boss acknowledged that Madueke has benefitted from his extra games at Arsenal due to Saka’s injury.

But Tuchel chose to keep the same front three that he had during the last international camp. The German wants to reward those players who have earned their place in the team and not make changes every time they are playing well at club level.

This means Madueke will be left frustrated, having to wait until next time.

Madueke has played under Tuchel before, so it shows how much he thinks of him as a player. He featured in England’s 2026 World Cup campaign, so it is noticeable that he was left out this time.

Although he can’t control the battle for places, he will now be able to continue building his case. With Saka still injured, he will have the chance to play more regularly and solidify his place in Arteta’s plans.

However, Madueke is not the only player at Arsenal who has had positive news.

Declan Rice, Eberechi Eze, Ezri Konsa and Myles Lewis-Skelly have all earned or retained their spots in Tuchel’s squad. Ben White also comes back into the England squad, having missed the last camp.

It is another chance for White to re-establish himself on the world stage after his knee injury kept him out of the World Cup.

It also means Arsenal have five players in the squad after Saka was left out due to injury.

England play Croatia at Wembley on November 12 before heading to Spain on November 15. Madueke will be watching from home, knowing he was almost part of the squad.

Now he needs to build on his recent form and become a consistent performer for Arsenal. Whilst Tuchel has left the door open, Madueke needs to give the England boss another reason to change his mind.

Reggie Watson Chelsea news continues to build, according to football.london, with the 16-year-old further establishing himself as one of Xabi Alonso’s wildcard options in the first-team squad. Watson has been named in both of Chelsea’s senior squads for their Carabao Cup ties this season.

He was an unused substitute in the second-round win over Luton Town, then made his full Chelsea debut in the 6-3 win over Leeds United at Stamford Bridge. Watson played the first 45 minutes against Leeds, a reward for his progress since Alonso took charge at Chelsea over the summer.

Every stage of Watson’s rise has come through football.london’s reporting, with Chelsea yet to make any official comment on his long-term role in the squad. The outlet’s football.london report traces how the teenager has moved from pre-season fixtures into genuine first-team contention this term.

Watson played a big part in Chelsea’s pre-season tour, completing the full 90 minutes of the opening match against Western Sydney Wanderers. He was also part of Chelsea’s travelling squad for the Brentford defeat before the international break. Watson was left out of the matchday squad list for that game, though, as explained in our Brentford line-up report.

Mahdi Nicoll-Jazuli was the Chelsea youngster named on the bench for that Brentford trip and came on late in the match, football.london added.

Watson’s form has caught attention away from the first-team picture too. He was one of three Chelsea youngsters nominated for Premier League 2’s Player of the Month award for September, alongside Chizaram Ezenwata and Leo Black, according to football.london.

Black recently signed a two-year contract at Chelsea after impressing on trial, while Watson built his own case by scoring three goals in September. The head coach has shown similar willingness to adapt his squad elsewhere, as detailed in Alonso’s recent rotation plans.

Chelsea are reported to be facing Portsmouth in a behind-closed-doors friendly this weekend, according to football.london, though the club has not confirmed the fixture publicly. Alonso reportedly wants to keep his squad sharp during an extended three-week international break.

Chelsea’s academy side, known as Cobham Next Gen, have no fixture of their own this weekend. That gap could leave Watson free to train or travel with the first-team squad again, football.london reports.

The teenager will try to impress Chelsea’s first-team staff in that friendly. Nothing is confirmed yet about what his involvement will be once the senior squad returns to action after the international break.

#2116. Arsenal transfer interest faces major obstacle as Santos secure £83m teenage talent

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Arsenal may have to rethink their interest in Santos forward Ignacio Pereira after the Brazilian club secured the teenager’s future with a substantial release clause.

The 16-year-old has caught the eye of a number of European clubs after an impressive campaign in Santos’ youth teams. The likes of Arsenal are said to have been watching his progress.

However, Santos have acted swiftly to secure the services of one of their most exciting young talents. Pereira signed his first professional contract with the club on November 4, tying him to the club until December 2029.

The deal also includes a 100 million euro release clause (around £83.4 million), according to Brazilian journalist Bruno Lima from UOL.

This poses a major problem for Arsenal if they are looking to sign the Argentine but also offers a lot of security for Santos, as they want to develop him as a first-team player.

Pereira has signed a new deal amid increasing interest in his development. Reports in Brazil have previously said that Arsenal, Real Betis, AC Milan, Lille, Standard Liège and Zenit are all keeping an eye on the rising star.

The club have now secured his services for another three years. The new agreement was awaiting registration with the Brazilian Football Confederation when the details emerged.

Pereira has spent three years at Santos, having joined from Juventude in 2023. He previously developed at Grêmio before moving to the São Paulo club.

Pereira has certainly lived up to the expectations. He scored an impressive 6 goals in 17 games in the Brazilian Under-17 Championship, making him the second-highest scorer for Santos. He also scored 4 goals in the Paulista Under-17 Championship.

This is very promising. It is his first season playing at under-17 level.

Pereira has been compared to Julián Álvarez due to his mobility and ability to play outside the box.

But the youngster cites another big name up front as his main inspiration. According to UOL, he cites Manchester City’s Erling Haaland as his inspiration, while Lionel Messi is still his football idol.

His profile suggests a versatile number nine who can play lower down the pitch, be an attacking midfielder and play with another striker. He has impressed the youth coaches at Santos with his finishing ability, physicality and desire to win.

He has a long way to go. Reports from Brazil suggest that Santos want to improve his tactical awareness and behaviour before making him more responsible.

He is being considered by the club for inclusion in their squad for the 2027 Copa São Paulo de Futebol Júnior (known as Copinha) and could start on the bench.

This interest from Arsenal does not necessarily mean that there is an imminent move for Pereira. The player is only 16, and a potential deal is made much more complicated due to a new contract.

Maybe the best course of action for Mikel Arteta’s recruitment team is to keep an eye on him. Santos has made it clear where they stand, and Pereira can now work towards his dream. Being in their first team.

#2115. Danny Drinkwater on Chelsea move he ‘could never turn down’

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

The Danny Drinkwater Chelsea story still carries a sting. Five years at Stamford Bridge gave him plenty of time to think about how it all turned out, and his own verdict is not a happy one.

Drinkwater opened up in a Truth or Padel appearance alongside David Ornstein. He said the move was one he could never have turned down, even with the benefit of hindsight. “No, I don’t regret moving. I regret, in hindsight, how it’s turned out. I think I could never turn it down,” he said.

Strip away the headlines and the numbers alone tell an uncomfortable story. Drinkwater spent five years on Chelsea’s books. In that time he made just 23 appearances, according to the original interview report.

Do the maths and it barely comes to five games a year. That is a thin return for a central midfielder signed to compete for a first-team place. It is also the kind of record that rarely sits well at a club of Chelsea’s size and ambition.

Chelsea’s best attacking spells over a similar stretch of seasons look far more productive, as our striker rankings show. Set next to those numbers, Drinkwater’s five quiet years stand out even more starkly.

Five years. Twenty three games. These numbers alone explains why this transfer still gets talked about by Drinkwater, long after the headlines faded.

The numbers only tell part of the story. A string of incidents turned a quiet, underwhelming spell into something far more pointed.

Drinkwater had a training-ground bust-up while out on loan. He was also involved in an on-pitch brawl with Chelsea’s own under-23s side. Flashpoints between a first-team player and a development squad are rare at any club, let alone one with Chelsea’s standards.

Taken alone, either incident might have passed as a bad day. Taken together, it became harder to see Drinkwater simply as an unlucky squad player who could not catch a break.

The most serious moment came during his time at Aston Villa. Drinkwater headbutted team-mate Jota and was fined two weeks’ wages for it. He says the consequences at Chelsea did not stop there.

“They didn’t formally ban me, but I got removed from the first team building after the incident at Villa,” Drinkwater said.

One off-field incident, at another club, pushed him out of the manager’s day-to-day plans back home. No formal punishment at Chelsea followed. The effect was much the same.

Chelsea fans saw a similar breakdown of trust play out when Graham Potter struggled to win over the dressing room during another difficult Chelsea transition. Trust can break down fast once results or behaviour turn against the person at the centre of it.

Drinkwater’s comments matter beyond his own career. They are a reminder that saying yes to a big move does not guarantee it will work once a player walks through the door.

That tension has not gone away. Chelsea’s current transfer business, covered in our transfer watch, shows the club still weighing big calls on players whose futures depend on more than form alone.

Squad management under Xabi Alonso, assessed in our bomb squad verdict, raises a familiar issue. Players who fall outside a manager’s plans rarely find a quick way back in, whether the exile is formal or not.

Drinkwater never found his way back into Chelsea’s plans after the Villa incident. His account of the Danny Drinkwater Chelsea years stands as a blunt warning. A move that looks impossible to refuse can still become one of the hardest spells of a career.

#2114. West Ham Defender’s Remarkable Numbers Reveal January Transfer Problem

A recent X post by Luke, better known as LouOrns, showed some Championship centre-back analysis revealing that despite our shaky defence, Konstantinos Mavropanos is doing things that almost nobody else in the division can.

Looking at defenders with over 700 minutes played, the data shows a league dominated by specialists. Some centre-backs win everything in the air but struggle to contribute in possession. Others are comfortable progressing the ball but lack the physical dominance required to thrive in the Championship. Mavropanos is a rare exception. Winning more than 83% of his aerial duels while also posting progressive passing numbers comfortably above the league average, the Greek international combines two qualities that are rarely found in the same player. Only Portsmouth’s Regan Poole comes close to matching that balance, underlining just how unique Mavropanos’ profile has become.

What makes Mavropanos particularly impressive is that he is far more than a defender who simply attacks long balls. His progressive passing numbers are comfortably above the Championship average.

While defenders such as Billy Koumetio (Charlton), Ben Cabango (Swansea) and Nasser Djiga (Wolves) provide a respectable balance, none can match the Greek’s combination of aerial superiority and distribution.

That balance is precisely what makes him such a valuable asset. In a division built on physical battles, second balls and direct football, having a centre-back who consistently wins first contact whilst also driving possession forward provides a clear competitive edge, though it needs another three defenders and a goalkeeper to follow suit!

Dinos has posted some seriously impressive numbers

The chart paints a simple picture: many defenders excel at one aspect of the game. Very few excel at both.

And the data still doesn’t tell the full story.

What the chart does not show is goals. Mavropanos has already contributed five goals this season, an outstanding return for a central defender and further evidence of his all-round impact.

The Greek colossus is one of the rare exceptions to the rule, a centre-back performing at the very highest Championship standard.

West Ham are expected to receive January bids, and with a season drifting towards mid-table obscurity, may struggle to persuade our one and only key defender to remain at the London Stadium.

#2113. Arsenal handed William Saliba boost as Arteta considers defender’s return

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

The William Saliba Arsenal boost comes at a crucial time. Saliba can use the November international break to build up his fitness after missing out on France’s latest squad.

Arteta confirmed that the defender had returned to full training ahead of Arsenal’s Champions League meeting with Slavia Prague. He also completed 45 minutes in a behind-closed-doors-friendly last week as he stepped up his recovery.

However, Saliba did not feature in the 2-1 victory. Arteta included him in the matchday squad for the first time this season, but the Frenchman remained an unused substitute.

It means he was not selected by Zinedine Zidane for France’s Nations League games against Italy and Turkey. This gives Arsenal a chance to continue working on his recovery. He will not be travelling for international games or playing in competitive matches.

Saliba has not featured since he suffered a back injury during France’s World Cup semi-final against Spain in July. Arteta has had to find alternatives in central defence in the early stages of the campaign.

Since transferring to Arsenal from Aston Villa in August, Konsa has played a part in filling the gap left by Saliba. His presence in the centre of defence complements Gabriel Magalhães and gives Arteta more options.

The England defender is also expected to be fit for the match against Hull City on Saturday. According to Arteta, the player’s replacement in the game against Slavia Prague was just a precaution after he reported some discomfort.

The fact that he has made a recovery means that Arsenal have the option to control how Saliba returns. Arteta has the possibility of bringing Saliba back gradually, as Konsa is ready to play and Mosquera is also available.

But Saliba’s return still matters. He needs to get back in shape before the fixtures start getting tougher after the international break.

Arteta has made it clear he will respect whatever decision Zidane makes regarding Saliba’s availability but acknowledges that he needs some game time after being out for so long.

France will play Italy on November 12 and then Turkey three days later. This means Saliba will be missing those matches, as Zidane named his 23-man squad on Friday.

This means he will be able to continue to be monitored by the club at their London Colney training ground. He won’t be able to return to play in international games ahead of his first appearance for the club.

Arsenal will be hoping that he can use the extra time to get himself ready for the next set of games, including a trip to Newcastle before playing Borussia Dortmund and Manchester City at home.

Arteta will be keen to have Saliba back in contention but will be focused on getting him back at the right time.

There is some immediate optimism on Konsa’s fitness for the game against Hull. But more importantly, Saliba has now progressed to a point where he can be included in the planning process.

#2112. Chelsea sweat on key stars as Bournemouth test looms

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Chelsea return to Premier League action this weekend with a growing treatment table and a lot of questions for Xabi Alonso to answer before Saturday’s visit of Bournemouth.

The main concern revolves around Joao Pedro. Chelsea’s current player of the season rescued a point against newly promoted Hull, but he suffered a knock and sat out the damaging 3-0 defeat at Brentford. The problem also prompted his removal from Brazil’s squad shortly after he had returned to it.

It is understood that he is undergoing specialist knee treatment in an attempt to expedite his recovery, although reports in Brazil suggest that he could be out for up to four weeks. No date has yet been set, but a return later in October looks most likely.

There is more good news elsewhere. Cole Palmer was among the players called up to the latest England squad, which did not go down too well with Thomas Tuchel.

The midfielder has been recovering from a persistent groin injury and has been working with a rehab coach. He is likely to be fit this weekend.

Moises Caicedo has also struggled with an injury this season, playing just 14 minutes across the campaign. He’s spent the majority of time on the bench recovering from a bad calf injury, which ruled him out for most of the season.

He made his only appearance as a substitute against his former club Brighton, but the game was red-flagged for Ecuador’s friendlies, so he did not play.

Alonso said before the Brentford match that the player would be ready to return. It seems to be the case, unless something went wrong.

Captain Reece James missed the Brentford game with yet another hamstring problem. Alonso said he only just missed out, so a return seems likely.

There may also be debuts. Marco Palestra, the £27m signing from Atalanta, has been sidelined since a training injury just before the opening match against Fulham. He is back in full training and was expected to feature in a behind-closed-doors friendly with Portsmouth. Chelsea lost 2-0. Emmanuel Emegha, injured after scoring against Bromley in July, is also pushing for involvement.

Less clear is the position of Jamie Gittens. He limped off with an ankle injury playing for England Under-21s against Kazakhstan and was seen on crutches. Mike Penders is fine after a quad scare on Belgium duty. Returning to the bench against Turkey.

Chelsea badly need a lift. Defeat at Brentford before the break left Alonso’s side looking shaky, and a squad stretched by injuries has not helped.

Saturday brings a chance to steady things. If Palmer, James and Caicedo are available, with Palestra and Emegha potentially adding fresh options, the picture will look much brighter at Stamford Bridge. Joao Pedro’s absence will still sting. However, a strong response against Bournemouth would go a long way towards easing the pressure and restoring belief.

#2111. GdS: The opportunity, the issues and the exes – why Genoa is a ‘true test’ for Amorim’s Milan

AC Milan take on Genoa this afternoon in need of a win to bounce back from a midweek defeat, which has become a familiar tale.

There is a lot riding on the game at the Stadio Luigi Ferraris for various reasons, but above all the feeling is that a positive result is required to make the international break less turbulent for Ruben Amorim and his side. La Gazzetta dello Sport have picked out a few angles in their preview.

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

Inter will host Como tonight: will Milan seize the day? That is the significance of the Rossoneri’s trip to Liguria. A win against Genoa would allow them to gain ground on Inter, Como, or both should the match at San Siro end in a draw.

As of this morning, Milan sit in fifth place with 21 points. If the season’s objective is a top-four finish, they must determine which team to target in the race. The top four are Lazio (28), Roma (27, having played one extra match), Inter (25) and Como (23).

Lazio is the big surprise, while the presence of the other three was to be expected. Milan won the derby, but this week’s European fixtures have re-established a gap: Inter defeated Feyenoord away in the Champions League, while Milan fell to Ferencváros at San Siro in the Europa League. Then there are Como, following their extraordinary 9-1 victory in RC Lens.

Yesterday, Roma eased past Sassuolo, ending the day in second place. Currently, Milan trail those ahead of them in the standings because – unlike the top four – they are still searching for a clear, strong identity.

Should Inter emerge victorious in Milano, a win in Genoa would allow the Diavolo to leapfrog Como and claim fourth place. So, Milan must take the opportunity, or risk further complications in their top four bid.

Inconsistency as a way of life for Milan. In their last six matches across Serie A and the Europa League, every victory has been followed by a defeat. Following this pattern of alternation, Milan should win today, having been beaten in Europe by Ferencváros on Thursday, but sequences are made to be broken.

Milan will only fully turn a corner and truly mature only when they step off this seesaw. To understand these ups and downs, one must look at the mindset. It is a psychological issue: the team do not know how to handle a win, treating it as a definitive cure only to suffer a relapse the very next time out.

Amorim is well aware of the problem: his disappointment following the Ferencváros match stemmed from the team’s attitude, which stood in stark contrast to the one displayed five days earlier against Inter. He addressed it in the dressing room (a polite way of saying he was furious).

It is unclear how much age plays a role, as Milan’s squad features a blend of youth and experience. For instance, in Thursday’s match against Ferencváros, both 20-year-old Cissé and 31-year-old Rabiot were equally ineffective.

Perhaps the question is whether there are enough figures capable of shaking up the group and keeping it alert and responsive. Judging by what is seen on the pitch, Mike Maignan possesses the strongest personality though one wonders whether his voice carries real weight.

Luka Modrić is a genius that has won it all, but he does not appear to be the type of player who rallies his team-mates, embodying instead a quiet, standard-setting form of leadership.

On Thursday, against Ferencváros, Gonçalo Ramos touched the ball 26 times over 90 minutes plus stoppage time. Among those who were not substituted, he was the least involved player for Milan.

While it is true that a centre-forward’s role naturally involves less ball possession than that of a midfielder or defender, what matters is that he remains a threat. Yet, against the Hungarians, Gonçalo was harmless, failing to register a single shot on target.

He was the Milan player who engaged in the highest number of duels (13), a statistic that reinforces the impression of a Ramos disconnected from the rest of the team, attempting to force individual solutions against multiple opponents.

So far, Ramos has appeared to be an ill-fitting piece in the side. In 13 matches across the league and the Europa League, he has scored just three goals: two in Serie A (one of which was a penalty) and one in Europe. He also has just one assist.

That is far too little for a €70m man. Gonçalo Ramos was not born a striker: he evolved into one, and he interprets the position in an atypical way, as a creative link-up player or an opportunist rather than a penalty box target man.

Much like Kolo Muani at Juventus, he functions more as a wide forward or a supplementary attacker than as an out-and-out goalscorer. There is no doubt that Gonçalo possesses refined technique, is capable of precise combination play, and excels at holding up the ball.

He started reasonably well at Milan, showing signs that he could blend flashes of brilliance with goalscoring. Gradually, however, he has faded, seemingly weighed down by a lack of confidence. It is surprising that Amorim is yet to fully unlock his qualities or integrate him effectively with the players around him.

Keep an eye on the contingent of former Milan players within the Genoa squad. Stephan El Shaarawy spent four years with the Diavolo between 2011 and 2015. Although he only won one Supercoppa Italiana, he left behind fond memories.

He is expected to start today. Daniele De Rossi has been deploying him between the left flank and the attacking midfield, where his signature drifting, inward-curving runs can pose a real threat.

Junior Messias is slated to begin on the bench, though whether that will actually be the case remains to be seen. The Brazilian spent two years in Milan, and was the standout performer in Genoa’s 2-1 victory over Lecce a week ago.

While Messias is somewhat injury-prone, he has managed to feature in every league match so far this season. Known for his creativity, he operates as a number 10 that is just as adept at setting up his team-mates as he is at scoring himself.

Lorenzo Colombo meanwhile is a product of the Milan youth academy and a favourite of Zlatan Ibrahimović, who once nicknamed him ‘Hulk’. He has seen his role in the Genoa attack diminish, as the Montenegrin player Osmajic has become the primary centre-forward.

That said, De Rossi has occasionally fielded them together to great effect. Colombo has yet to score in this Serie A campaign, though he has provided two assists, both for Osmajic.

Notably absent from the Genoa squad is the talented 18-year-old Brazilian Robinho Junior, son of the Robinho who won the 2010-11 Scudetto with Milan.

#2110. Chelsea co-sporting director Laurence Stewart to leave in leadership reshuffle

Gary Gowers is a sports journalist and the editor of Read Everton, where he leads daily coverage of Everton news, transfers and analysis. He also writes about Manchester City for Read Man City and motorsport for Read Motorsport. A former accountant, he became a freelance writer in 2011 and has written for BBC Sport and Metro.

Laurence Stewart is set to leave Chelsea by mutual consent after almost four years at Stamford Bridge, with the club opting to redistribute his responsibilities rather than appoint a direct replacement.

The departure is understood to be amicable and won’t take immediate effect. Stewart could continue attending matches before formally leaving, following discussions with co-owner Behdad Eghbali over the club’s evolving sporting structure.

The story was first reported by Matt Law of The Telegraph and Nizaar Kinsella of BBC Sport, with Ben Jacobs subsequently providing further details about how Chelsea intend to operate.

Stewart arrived from Monaco in October 2022 as technical director before becoming co-sporting director alongside Paul Winstanley in February 2023.

Chelsea’s day-to-day sporting leadership will now be shared between Winstanley, Sam Jewell and Joe Shields, each with defined responsibilities.

Winstanley will continue overseeing transfer negotiations, player contracts and several departments at Cobham, including medical and performance operations. Shields’ work centres on the academy and player pathways, while Jewell has wider responsibilities across the BlueCo operation, including Strasbourg.

According to Jacobs, discussions about restructuring began in April and accelerated following the appointment of Xabi Alonso as head coach.

Stewart had overseen investment in Chelsea’s data analysis operation, although Jewell and Shields have increasingly taken responsibility for using that information in recruitment and talent identification.

Dave Fallows will remain involved in an advisory capacity, rather than taking on a fixed day-to-day portfolio.

The changes are being described as a natural evolution of Chelsea’s sporting model. There is no suggestion that Stewart’s departure followed a breakdown in relations, and the club are not currently planning to recruit a replacement.

#2109. Arsenal transfer news: Arsenal set January transfer priorities as Fabrizio Romano delivers Piero Hincapié update

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

In Arsenal transfer news, Arsenal could approach the January transfer window with a different focus after Fabrizio Romano revealed that Mikel Arteta has no intention of weakening his defensive options.

Piero Hincapié has been linked with a move to Barcelona. There have been reports that a deal could be done involving Alejandro Balde, but Romano expects the Ecuadorian to stay at Emirates Stadium.

Arsenal have been strengthening their defensive department under Arteta. Hincapié only made the switch permanently from Bayer Leverkusen in the summer after joining on loan.

Despite competition, the 24-year-old is a key player in Arteta’s plans. His versatility to play in either a centre back or left back role gives Arteta a valuable option.

However, we can also gain insight into where Arsenal should target their recruitment from Romano’s latest comments. Instead of changing their defensive options, it could be an opportunity to improve their attacking options.

Despite competition, the 24-year-old is a key player in Arteta’s plans. His versatility to play in either a centre back or left back role gives Arteta a valuable option.

However, we can also gain insight into where Arsenal should target their recruitment from Romano’s latest comments. Instead of changing their defensive options, it could be an opportunity to improve their attacking options.

However, Arsenal have little incentive to let him leave halfway through the season, as losing a two-in-one defender will cause problems for Arteta, given how much they need to do in multiple competitions.

Romano’s comments suggest that Arsenal are happy with their existing defensive unit and don’t want to cause any disruption to their balance. He also suggested that Hincapié is an important player for them, meaning he’s unlikely to leave in January.

That leaves Barcelona needing to explore alternative targets if they want to strengthen their backline. A deal involving Balde and Hincapié does not currently appear to be a realistic option.

The more interesting part of Romano’s report is Arsenal’s potential business at the other end of the field.

He feels they will be looking to make a move if the right attacking player comes up for grabs. But he doesn’t think they will make a signing just because they can in January.

This highlights the issues clubs face with mid-season transfers. Often selling clubs will be unwilling to lose key players, and finding suitable replacements can be tough.

As such, Arsenal are likely to look at the market to see what they can do. However, it looks like there could still be a forward brought in.

This is an important distinction. The point being made here is that whilst there is speculation around one particular player, Romano has indicated that Arsenal can strengthen their attacking options. This is not to say that any particular move is happening.

If Arteta keeps Hincapié, then Arsenal will maintain their defensive stability and possibly gain another option in attack.

Over the next few weeks we will get a better understanding of what is available to them. But for now it looks like Arsenal are not keen to lose Hincapié but will look to strengthen their attacking options if the right deal comes along.

#2108. West Ham Insider Reveals Recruitment Mess Behind Club’s Decline

West Ham United are still suffering because of years of bad recruitment, Claret & Hugh has been told by a Hammers insider today. The source, who did not wish to be named, described the situation as a mess and laid the blame for the club’s current predicament firmly and squarely at muddled, poor recruitment.

Speaking to Claret & Hugh, the source said:

“It’s all down to recruitment and it’s been bad, a real mess.”

“It’s a hodge podge with players being brought from everywhere by loads of different people. We’re years behind and needed a complete reset, we had to start again and consider how we recruit.”

Honest words, and not ones that many West Ham fans would disagree with. The club has spent years changing managers, recruitment chiefs and transfer strategies, seemingly without allowing any one approach enough time to work. The result is a squad assembled under several different regimes, with little evidence of a consistent long-term plan.

It has always been Daniel Křetínský’s wish at West Ham to have a director of football in charge, and whilst we don’t have that exact job title at the club currently, Nils Koppen, as Director of Player Recruitment, is as good as a director of football in all but name.

What is clear is that the club are going to have to rebuild the recruitment structure brick by brick. And it’s worth pointing out that even as recently as the summer, there was still something of a mix-up, with a blend of recruitment ideas and Nuno Espírito Santo being allowed to bring in three players in Joël Piroe, Manor Solomon and Morato.

Kretinsky is a big believer in the structured recruitment model

That is hardly the definition of a joined-up recruitment strategy, although it’s also important to recognise that Koppen arrived with very little time to prepare for the summer window. The bigger issue is whether West Ham are finally prepared to commit to a consistent approach rather than changing direction whenever the manager changes.

We’re semi-lucky in one respect because we do have another transfer window coming up in January, giving Koppen and Scott Parker an opportunity to address some of the more obvious problems within the squad.

But this is definitely a time when West Ham have to commit to a transfer policy and allow it to play out and bear fruit over a number of seasons. Toying with an approach and then giving up, as we did with both Tim Steidten and Kyle Macaulay, is precisely the sort of short-term thinking that has contributed to the problem.

The club cannot keep appointing recruitment specialists, allowing them to start rebuilding the squad and then abandoning the entire project when results turn sour. At some point, West Ham have to decide what sort of players they want, how they’re going to identify them and who is ultimately responsible for bringing them in.

January offers an opportunity to begin correcting some of the mistakes, but the problems described by our source won’t be fixed in a single transfer window. If West Ham are genuinely years behind in their recruitment, the rebuild needs to be given the time and consistency that previous attempts have lacked.

Kris Gonzo (commonly known to the West Ham community as Gonzo) is the co-founder of Hammers Chat (established 2014) and the lead video content creator and columnist for Claret and Hugh.

With over a decade of professional sports media experience, he is a frequent West Ham United analyst and contributor for Sky Sports News, BBC Football Focus, and BBC Radio 5 Live. He previously served as an official video and content creator for West Ham United FC during the club's tenure at Upton Park and featured in the official documentary commemorating the historic final game at the Boleyn Ground.

A lifelong Hammer since the legendary 10-0 victory over Bury, when he isn't providing daily match analysis on the Hammers Chat YouTube Channel or broadcasting on X (@GONZObignose), he can be found walking his dog and restoring classic pinball machines.

I think the main problem has been David Sullivan’s autocratic dominance and net work of his agents. Let us hope we are now free of this and have a balanced outlook

Where’s all this money coming from to fund the (start of) this overhaul. I can’t recall all the figures now but debt in excess of £200m, some of which had to (and has been) repaid, losing £100m a year and whilst wages have reduced by, say 50%, so has revenue. Unless Kretinsky is now a 45% and the major shareholder ( there appears to be conflicting info as to whether this transaction has completed) he is, based on history, little chance of him providing a transfer kitty and even if he is how does ‘FFP’ come into the equation?

I don’t think we needed a club insider to tell us about this issue. Yes not all signings will work out, but our success rate has been particularly poor in the last 3 years. I am not against a manager making suggestions as you allude to with Nuno, but they must be agreed by all relevant parties, then it becomes a cooperative relationship between manager and DOF.

The issue is that poor recruitment rarely costs directly on the books. Players amortise and those that don’t work out at all go out on loan bringing in loan fees.

The real problem lies in the contributions that they do not make. We invest and don’t lose out on the books but we do lose out in having invested in a player who did not contribute for us.

That affects results so affects success so affects us financially in that respect. Repeated poor investments.

It seems a shame that Daniel Kretinsky was not exerting influence at the time when David Moyes was also trying to build a consistent team.

We had it all then. We needed to continue with the Jarrod Bowen type of recruitment. We needed to back the manager and his coaching staff.

Instead, we were treated to exotic ideas of radical change which turned out to be a waste of money, time and a football club.

Tim Steidten could not believe his luck and bank balance as he scoured the world for excuses to spend on as high a profile as possible players to add to and become the basis of his team. No thoughts as to how these players would work in the game plans with the other players we already had. Only a passing reference to the team coach as the stars were aligned. The manager’s fault when success on the field was not forthcoming.

Poor recruitment played its part but continuity and investment in building upon a solid foundation was the real problem.

Let us hope that Scott Parker is allowed to continue to build our new team and not just until a more exotic name like Pellegrini or Lopetegui gets whispered into the ears of Daniel Kretinsky.

We have an opportunity now. We must take it.

#2107. Chelsea v Bournemouth: Kick-off time, TV details and how to follow

Gary Gowers is a sports journalist and the editor of Read Everton, where he leads daily coverage of Everton news, transfers and analysis. He also writes about Manchester City for Read Man City and motorsport for Read Motorsport. A former accountant, he became a freelance writer in 2011 and has written for BBC Sport and Metro.

There will be no live UK television coverage of Chelsea’s return to Premier League action against Bournemouth on Saturday afternoon.

Xabi Alonso’s side host the Cherries at Stamford Bridge in a traditional 3pm kick-off, with the fixture falling within the Saturday afternoon broadcasting blackout.

That means supporters unable to attend will need to follow the match through other channels, rather than watching it live on Sky Sports or TNT Sports.

Although the game will not be televised live in the UK, supporters can follow developments through the official Chelsea website and the Premier League website.

The Saturday 3pm blackout prevents live domestic television broadcasts during the restricted period, although overseas coverage may be available through broadcasters holding the relevant rights.

Highlights will be available after the match through the usual broadcast and digital channels, subject to their respective release schedules.

Chelsea will then turn their attention to a trip to Everton on Saturday, 17 October, with that fixture scheduled for a 12.30pm kick-off at Hill Dickinson Stadium.

For Alonso, the Bournemouth match provides the immediate opportunity to resume Chelsea’s domestic campaign following the international break.

#2106. Arsenal vs Hull City preview: Arteta sticks with winning formula as Gunners target another three points

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Arsenal have resisted the temptation to rotate after their victory at Anfield, with Mikel Arteta naming the same starting XI for Saturday’s meeting with Hull City.

Arsenal welcome Championship side Hull City to the Emirates Stadium, looking to strengthen their grip on the top spot after collecting 24 points from their opening nine Premier League games. However, their tally of just 14 goals suggests Arteta’s side still have work to do in the final third.

Arteta has again chosen to partner Ezri Konsa and Gabriel Magalhães in defence. William Saliba was left out despite pre-match talk that he would be named in the starting XI. Bukayo Saka and Max Dowman are also missing from the squad.

Noni Madueke will carry the right. Tzolis starts on the opposite flank. Kai Havertz will lead the line. Viktor Gyökeres, Eberechi Eze and Mikel Merino add attacking options from the bench.

Arsenal predicted starting XI: Raya; Timber, Konsa, Gabriel, Calafiori; Guimarães, Rice, Ødegaard; Madueke, Havertz, Tzolis.

Substitutes: Arrizabalaga, White, Mosquera, Hincapié, Lewis-Skelly, Zubimendi, Merino, Eze, Gyökeres.

Hull are coming into this game ranked 11th in the league after five draws in their first nine games. They’ve not won in their last eight games but can cause problems for any opposition.

They look like playing a defensive game with Slater, Iroegbunam and Gourna-Douath in the midfield to protect the backline. McBurnie and Cho will give them some attacking options.

Hull starting XI: Tzolakis; Coyle, Ajayi, Egan, Mendy, Stroud; Slater, Iroegbunam, Gourna-Douath; McBurnie, Cho.

Substitutes: Butland, Giles, Thomas, Gelhardt, Hjerto-Dahl, Norton-Cuffy, Herrington, Mouzakitis, Vaz.

McBurnie is just one booking away from suspension for the clash against Brighton on November 22 but may make an important impact in the physical battle with Gabriel.

Hull have not scored at the Emirates since 2014 but will look to turn that around. However, their main concern will be to ensure they keep Arsenal quiet for as long as possible.

Arsenal will need to find ways to break down the likely tight Hull backline.

Ødegaard and Rice will be key in keeping possession and creating chances, whilst Madueke and Tzolis will create width and movement.

Hull may be exposed to threat in case they are trapped in defence for too long, but Arteta can use Gyökeres if Havertz is having difficulties doing anything productive.

A win for Arsenal will give them a six-point lead. Manchester City will be looking to get all three points when they face Nottingham Forest later on Saturday.

The visitors will look to create an uncomfortable afternoon for the Gunners.

Arsenal have enough quality to take all three points, but Hull will not make it easy for them. I expect this to be a tight game, and the Gunners will win by a small margin.

#2105. West Ham Exclusive: Hammers Open To Castellanos Sale As Koppen Targets Striker

Claret & Hugh can exclusively reveal today that West Ham United are exploring the possibility of selling striker Taty Castellanos during the January transfer window. However, the chances of Everton returning for the Argentine have been described as “unlikely” by a football agent speaking to C&H.

Everton were reported to have made a deadline-day bid at the end of the summer transfer window, but with West Ham unable to source a replacement in time, the offer was rejected. The Premier League club’s interest was substantial, although there is currently no indication that David Moyes will renew his pursuit.

It would be fair to say that Castellanos has been something of a disappointment in the Championship this season, and West Ham are now actively looking to recruit another forward when the window opens in January. Director of Player Recruitment Nils Koppen has already begun making enquiries to agents and clubs as the Hammers prepare to strengthen Scott Parker’s squad.

Koppen wants to sign a striker in January

Speaking to Claret & Hugh this morning, our source confirmed that the club’s search for a new centre-forward is already underway.

“Nils has already made an approach for one striker in France.”

When asked whether there could also be departures from West Ham’s attacking department, the agent added:

“West Ham would sell Castellanos if an offer came in.”

The source did not specify how much West Ham would require for the Argentine, nor did they identify the striker Koppen has approached. However, the comments suggest that the club are prepared to consider a significant change to their attacking options in January.

Castellanos cost West Ham £27 million when he arrived from Lazio in January, but his performances in the Championship have fallen well short of expectations. The striker’s difficulties have been evident throughout the campaign, with his lack of goals already raising serious questions about his future.

Everton’s previous interest was first reported in September, when it emerged that West Ham had been prepared to consider a substantial offer but could not find a suitable replacement before the deadline.

The possibility of a January departure has also been discussed previously, although today’s information provides a fresh indication that the club are actively exploring their options.

It will come as little surprise to anybody who has watched Castellanos this season that West Ham would consider moving him on. The Argentine has struggled to make the impact expected of a £27 million striker, and Parker is now beginning the process of assessing what he needs from his squad.

Whether a suitable offer arrives remains to be seen, particularly with a return from Everton considered unlikely by our source. But with Koppen already approaching potential replacements, it appears West Ham are preparing for the possibility of a significant change up front when the January window opens.

#2104. Arsenal 1-0 Hull City: Martin Ødegaard rescues Gunners with late winner at the Emirates

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Arsenal needed a moment of quality from their captain to overcome Hull City and claim a narrow 1-0 Premier League victory at the Emirates Stadium.

Martin Ødegaard scored in the 72nd minute as the hosts struggled to break down a resolute Hull side. It was much like I expected in my match preview, as Arsenal had to work hard to win this game.

The result gives them an extended lead at the top of the table. Ahead of Manchester City’s clash with Nottingham Forest. However, Mikel Arteta will be aware that his team did not play well in this game. It was far from a walk in the park.

Hull arrived at London looking to play an attacking game. Jakirovic’s side were well organised and looked to launch quick counter attacks.

They did have some positive moments in the first half, but Arsenal struggled to get into their attacking rhythm. Kai Havertz couldn’t capitalise on one good chance created by Ødegaard.

The hosts were hopeful of taking the lead after Havertz fell to the ground in the box after being pulled back by Nobel Mendy.

However, referee Farai Hallam’s initial decision was overturned after a VAR review showed Havertz was offside earlier in the attack, so Arsenal’s penalty was cancelled.

Another frustrating moment for the home side, who failed to score during the first half, despite dominating possession.

Arsenal came out strong after the break. However, Hull were still causing problems. Lewie Coyle was also walking a fine line after a yellow card in the first half and a late challenge on Christos Tzolis.

Arteta made a three-man change on the hour mark, bringing on Viktor Gyökeres and Mikel Merino for Havertz and Declan Rice. Six minutes later he brought on Eberechi Eze and Piero Hincapié.

The breakthrough came from Madueke, forcing Konstantinos Tzolakis into action. The Hull keeper couldn’t hold his shot, and Ødegaard was quickest to react, scoring from the rebound.

The captain’s strike took the frustration out of Arsenal’s efforts and was a reward for their perseverance. It also provided another vital moment for the Norwegian. He’s had a tough campaign so far due to injuries.

Despite going behind, Hull never gave up in their search for an equaliser. However, Arsenal held on to their lead.

Arsenal dominated the game with 67% of possession and 21 shots, compared to just seven for Hull. Tzolakis put in a number of key interventions to keep the scoreline respectable.

Although they weren’t at their best, Arsenal were able to perform in the key moments of the game. Ødegaard’s goal meant that the Gunners earned three points and are sitting top of the Premier League heading into the international break.
